> description: A relentless interview to sharpen a plan, design, or topic, checkpointing every answer to a brainstorm file so nothing is lost. < Call the Skill tool with "grilling".
---
> # Grill Me
> 
> One skill, self-contained. It holds both the **interview method** (Step 2) and the **capture discipline** (Steps 1 and 3): every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up. It does not call the vendor `grilling` skill: the method is written inline below so this skill can add the capture steps around it and keep working even if `grilling` changes upstream.
> 
> If the topic is a feature, component, or other implementation work rather than an open-ended plan or decision, `brainstorming` runs a similar interview but gates on a written, approved design doc before any code — reach for that instead when the destination is implementation.
> 
> ## Step 1: set up the capture file (BEFORE the first question)
> 
> 1. Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`; in an established project follow its declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.
> 2. Create the capture file at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create `brainstorms/` if missing). Every capture lives here, one predictable home regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. A polished deliverable (plan, map, spec) may later move into the relevant `projects/` folder; the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
>    - Get today's real date from the clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
>    - Never overwrite an existing capture. Same date and topic: add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user asks or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
>    - No topic supplied or inferable: create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, ask what to explore, record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
> 3. Write the header immediately: title, date, goal of the session, empty "Open flags" section.
> 4. Tell the user where you are saving, in one line.
> 
> ## Step 2: run the interview
> 
> Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.
> 
> Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you have not heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.
> 
> Format a round like so:
> 
> ```
> **Q1 - <question title>**: <question body, may be multiple paragraphs, including choices>
> 
> Recommended: <your recommended answer>
> 
> ---
> 
> **Q2 - <question title>**: ...
> 
> Recommended: ...
> ```
> 
> Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.
> 
> Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, live site), look it up or dispatch a sub-agent; never ask the user for anything you could find yourself. Do not block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.
> 
> The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch visited, nothing silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.
> 
> Two adjustments the capture layer imposes on the method:
> 
> - For **decisions**, give a recommended answer grounded in context and label it as a recommendation. For **personal or business facts**, ask neutrally. Never lead with invented facts, and never save your inference as the user's answer.
> - When the user **cannot answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Do not stall the round on it.
> 
> ## Step 3: the checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)
> 
> After EVERY round of answers, BEFORE you ask the next round:
> 
> - Append one structured entry per answered question: the question, the key facts and decisions from the answer (in the user's words where wording matters), and any flags (things they could not answer, plus who should).

51:| grill-with-docs-codex | Same direction with context, glossary, ADRs | Inverse direction with context, glossary, ADRs |
53:Codex entry-point names identify Claude as the delegate. The updater maps upstream names to these installed names and retires the old Codex-side copies into inactive backups. Explicit user provider/model choices still take precedence in the general loop and route. The additional grill-by-claude shortcut is intended for a Codex host.
104:## 2026-09-16: grill-with-docs activation repair
106:The ordinary grill-with-docs, grilling, and domain-modeling skills are activated as complete project copies in both runtimes. Codex loads the two sibling SKILL.md files directly, with explicit-only discovery in agents/openai.yaml. Claude also reads sibling files directly because a dependency can disable model invocation. This is a runtime invocation adaptation, not a single-runtime skill exception. The separate Claude-only grill-with-docs-codex entry remains unchanged.
108:## 2026-09-26: grill-with-docs back to upstream; grill-me kept as a local rewrite
110:Supersedes the 2026-09-16 note above. The sibling-file workaround existed only because a local edit had added `disable-model-invocation: true` to `grilling`, which upstream (mattpocock/skills) never carries. That line is removed from every copy, so upstream `grill-with-docs` ("call the Skill tool twice, for grilling and domain-modeling") works again and is now installed verbatim in every runtime. The daily sync (`system/scripts/sync-mattpocock-skills.py`) keeps it current.
112:`grill-me` is deliberately NOT upstream in the Claude and Gemini copies (global, `skills/claude`, `skills/gemini`). Upstream's `grill-me` is a one-line call to `grilling`; Joshua's version adds the capture discipline (every answer checkpointed to a brainstorm file) that his brainstorm and AI OS context sessions depend on. Decided by Joshua 2026-09-26. The sync script lists it in `KEEP` and never overwrites it. The Codex copy is upstream.
128:- From Codex use `grill-me-claude` for an interview followed by independent review, `grill-with-docs-claude` for persistent context/glossary/ADRs, `claude-review` for an existing work product, and `claude-build` for delegated implementation. From Claude use the corresponding `*-codex` triggers. Generic `grilling` is the interview without cross-provider review; `grill-with-docs` combines that interview with domain modeling. Codex legacy `grill-me-codex` and `grill-with-docs-codex` remain explicit compatibility wrappers to their Claude-review counterparts.

def fetch_upstream():
    sha = subprocess.check_output(["gh", "api", f"repos/{REPO}/commits/main", "--jq", ".sha"], text=True).strip()
    raw = subprocess.check_output(["gh", "api", f"repos/{REPO}/tarball/{sha}"])
    skills = {}
    with tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(raw)) as tf:
        for m in tf.getmembers():
            if not m.isfile():
                continue
            parts = m.name.split("/")[1:]  # drop the tarball's top folder
            if len(parts) >= 4 and parts[0] == "skills":
                skill_dir = "/".join(parts[:3])  # skills/<group>/<name>
                skills.setdefault(skill_dir, {})["/".join(parts[3:])] = tf.extractfile(m).read()
    return sha, skills

---
# Changes to `misc/` skills

The skills under `skills/misc/` (`git-guardrails-claude-code`, `migrate-to-shoehorn`, `scaffold-exercises`, `setup-pre-commit`, and anything else that lands there) are frozen. Bug reports, feature requests and PRs against them are closed.

## Why this is out of scope

`misc/` is where skills go when I keep them around but rarely use them. They aren't promoted: they don't ship in the Claude Code plugin, they have no docs page, and I'm not running them day to day, so I can't tell whether a fix is right or notice when one breaks something else. Maintaining them at the same bar as the promoted skills would cost the same as a promoted skill for a fraction of the use.

They stay in the repo, unmaintained, because they still work for some people as-is. If one doesn't work for you, install it with skills.sh and fix your copy: the files are yours to edit.

If a `misc/` skill is promoted back into `engineering/` or `productivity/` later, it's back in scope from that point.

- #465: "git-guardrails-claude-code documentation is missing benefits over built in Claude code deny rules"
- #898: "git-guardrails-claude-code: hook fails open without jq and misses common git spellings"

    border-radius: var(--r);
  }

  .chain h2 {
    font-family: var(--display);
    font-weight: 500;
    font-size: 1.32rem;
    margin: 0 0 4px;
    letter-spacing: -0.01em;
  }

  .chain p.sub { margin: 0 0 18px; color: var(--muted); font-size: 14.5px; max-width: 62ch; }

  .chainflow {
    display: flex;
    align-items: stretch;
    gap: 0;
    overflow-x: auto;
    padding-bottom: 6px;
  }

  .step {
    flex: 1 1 0;
    min-width: 132px;
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
    gap: 5px;
    padding: 0 14px;
    border-left: 2px solid var(--accent-line);
    cursor: pointer;
    background: none;
    border-top: 0; border-right: 0; border-bottom: 0;
    text-align: left;
    font-family: var(--body);
    color: inherit;
  }

  .step:first-child { padding-left: 0; border-left: 0; }
  .step:hover .step-name { color: var(--accent); }
  .step:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: 3px; }

  .step-n {
    font-family: var(--mono);
    font-size: 10.5px;
    color: var(--faint);
    letter-spacing: 0.1em;
  }

  .step-name {
    font-family: var(--mono);
    font-size: 13px;
    font-weight: 500;
    color: var(--ink);
    transition: color .12s;
  }

  .step-what { font-size: 12.5px; color: var(--muted); line-height: 1.4; }

  /* ---------- sections ---------- */

  section.group { margin-top: 46px; scroll-margin-top: 120px; }

  .grouphead {
    display: flex;
    align-items: baseline;
    gap: 12px;
    margin-bottom: 4px;
  }

  .grouphead h2 {
    font-family: var(--display);
    font-weight: 500;
    font-size: 1.62rem;
    letter-spacing: -0.015em;
    margin: 0;
  }

  .grouphead .n {
    font-family: var(--mono);
    font-size: 11.5px;
    color: var(--faint);
    font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  }

  .groupsub { margin: 0 0 18px; color: var(--muted); font-size: 14.5px; max-width: 64ch; }

  .cards { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(320px, 1fr)); gap: 12px; }

  /* ---------- card ---------- */

  .card {
    background: var(--surface);
    border: 1px solid var(--line);
    border-radius: var(--r);
    overflow: hidden;
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
  }

  .card.is-open { border-color: var(--accent-line); }

  .cardbtn {
    width: 100%;
    text-align: left;
    background: none;
    border: 0;
    padding: 15px 16px;
    cursor: pointer;
    font-family: var(--body);
    color: inherit;
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
    gap: 7px;
  }

  .cardbtn:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: -2px; }

  .cardtop { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 8px; flex-wrap: wrap; }

  .cmd {
    font-family: var(--mono);
    font-size: 13.5px;
    font-weight: 500;
    color: var(--ink);
  }

  .tag {
    font-family: var(--mono);
    font-size: 9.5px;
    letter-spacing: 0.08em;
    text-transform: uppercase;
    padding: 2.5px 6px;
    border-radius: 2px;
    white-space: nowrap;
  }

  .tag.yours { background: var(--yours-tint); color: var(--yours); }
  .tag.scope { background: var(--surface-2); color: var(--muted); }
  .tag.type { background: var(--accent-tint); color: var(--accent); }

  .what { font-size: 14.5px; line-height: 1.5; color: var(--muted); margin: 0; }

  .caret {
    margin-left: auto;
    font-family: var(--mono);
    font-size: 15px;
    color: var(--faint);
    line-height: 1;
    transition: transform .16s ease;
    flex: none;
  }

  .card.is-open .caret { transform: rotate(45deg); }

  .detail {
    display: none;
    padding: 0 16px 16px;
    flex-direction: column;
    gap: 12px;
    border-top: 1px solid var(--line);
    margin-top: 0;
    padding-top: 14px;
  }

  .card.is-open .detail { display: flex; }

  .example {
    background: var(--surface-2);
    border-left: 2px solid var(--accent);
    padding: 10px 13px;
    border-radius: 0 var(--r) var(--r) 0;
  }

  .example .lbl {
    font-family: var(--mono);
    font-size: 9.5px;
    letter-spacing: 0.1em;
    text-transform: uppercase;
    color: var(--faint);
    display: block;
    margin-bottom: 4px;
  }

  .example p { margin: 0; font-size: 14px; line-height: 1.5; font-style: italic; color: var(--ink); }

  .note { font-size: 13.5px; line-height: 1.5; color: var(--muted); margin: 0; }
  .note b { color: var(--ink); font-weight: 600; }

  .empty {
    padding: 56px 0;
    text-align: center;
    color: var(--faint);
    font-family: var(--display);
    font-size: 1.15rem;
  }

  footer {
    margin-top: 64px;
    padding-top: 22px;
    border-top: 1px solid var(--line);
    color: var(--faint);
    font-size: 13px;
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
    gap: 6px;
  }

  footer a { color: var(--accent); text-decoration: none; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--accent-line); }
  footer a:hover { border-bottom-color: var(--accent); }

  @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
    * { transition: none !important; animation: none !important; }
  }

  @media (max-width: 620px) {
    header.masthead { padding: 40px 0 28px; }
    .cards { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }
  }
</style>

<div class="wrap">

  <header class="masthead">
    <p class="eyebrow">30 installed &middot; 4 work everywhere &middot; auto-updating</p>
    <h1>The Skill Bench</h1>
    <p class="standfirst">Every skill on your machine, in plain words, with an example of something you'd actually say to trigger it. Tap any card to open it. <b>Everywhere</b> means it works in any folder; the rest are active in this vault and in LSAT&nbsp;U.</p>
  </header>

  <div class="controls">
    <div class="searchrow">
      <label class="search">
        <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" aria-hidden="true"><circle cx="11" cy="11" r="7"/><path d="M20 20l-3.5-3.5"/></svg>
        <input id="q" type="search" placeholder="Search: writing, bug, plan, git&hellip;" autocomplete="off" aria-label="Search skills">
      </label>
      <span class="count" id="count"></span>
    </div>
    <div class="chips" id="chips" role="group" aria-label="Filter by category"></div>
  </div>

  <div class="chain">
    <h2>The build chain</h2>
    <p class="sub">These five run in order, and only in a codebase. Each hands its conversation to the next, so you never start over. This is the one place where sequence actually matters.</p>
    <div class="chainflow" id="chainflow"></div>
  </div>

  <main id="groups"></main>

  <footer>
    <div>Skills by <a href="https://github.com/mattpocock/skills">Matt Pocock</a> (MIT), except <span style="color:var(--yours);font-weight:600">grill-me</span>, which is his interview plus Nate Herk's brainstorm file and your popups.</div>
    <div>Updates pull automatically every Monday and Thursday. Changes get logged to <span style="font-family:var(--mono)">wiki/upstream-updates.md</span>.</div>
  </footer>

</div>

<script>
const SKILLS = [
  // ---- Thinking it through ----
  { id:"grill-me", cat:"Thinking", scope:"everywhere", yours:true, type:"you type it",
    what:"Interviews you about an idea and saves every answer to a file, so nothing is lost.",
    ex:"“Grill me on how the Activity Centre should work.”",
    note:"<b>Yours.</b> The only one that writes a brainstorm file. Use it for business, teaching and product thinking: anything that isn't code. It saves to <b>brainstorms/</b> and warns you first if it started itself." },
  { id:"grill-with-docs", cat:"Thinking", type:"you type it",
    what:"The same interview, but inside a codebase. It writes a glossary and decision records as you go.",
    ex:"“/grill-with-docs” before changing something in LSAT U.",
    note:"<b>You must type this one.</b> No agent can start it for you. It writes CONTEXT.md (a glossary, nothing else) and ADRs for big decisions. No brainstorm file." },
  { id:"grilling", cat:"Thinking", type:"engine",
    what:"The interview itself. Asks a batch of related questions at once instead of dripping them one at a time.",
    ex:"You rarely type this. The two above run it for you.",
    note:"Every question comes with its recommended answer, so you can reply “1 yes, 2 the second one, 3 no”. It looks up facts itself instead of asking you things it could find." },
  { id:"wayfinder", cat:"Thinking", type:"you type it",
    what:"For work too big for one sitting. Maps out everything still to be decided, then works through it over days.",
    ex:"“/wayfinder” for a whole new bootcamp, not a single feature.",
    note:"It plans, it never builds. Needs an issue tracker, so run it in <b>lsatu</b>, not the vault. If you could finish in one sitting, use grill-with-docs instead." },
  { id:"prototype", cat:"Thinking",
    what:"Builds a throwaway version to answer a question that talking can't settle.",
    ex:"“Should this be one long page or three steps? Build both.”",
    note:"For questions about how something <b>looks or feels</b>. You can't decide those by discussing them, so stop grilling and go look at it." },
  { id:"to-questionnaire", cat:"Thinking",
    what:"Turns a question you can't answer into a form for someone else to fill in.",
    ex:"“I don't know what students find hardest here, so make me something to send them.”" },
  { id:"ask-matt", cat:"Thinking", scope:"everywhere",
    what:"Tells you which skill fits your situation.",
    ex:"“I'm stuck at the end of a session, what should I run?”",
    note:"The one to use when this page is too long. Just ask it." },

  // ---- Building ----
  { id:"to-spec", cat:"Building",
    what:"Turns the conversation you just had into a written spec. No new questions.",
    ex:"Right after a grilling session: “now turn this into a spec.”",
    note:"<b>This is the answer to “where does the rest go?”</b> A grill only writes down terms and big decisions. Everything else lives in the chat, so don't clear it, run this." },
  { id:"to-tickets", cat:"Building",
    what:"Breaks a plan into individual tickets, in the order they have to happen.",
    ex:"“Split this spec into tickets I can work through.”" },
  { id:"implement", cat:"Building",
    what:"Actually writes the code, working from the spec or tickets.",
    ex:"“Build ticket 3.”" },
  { id:"code-review", cat:"Building", scope:"built in",
    what:"Reviews what changed for real bugs, and can fix them or post them on the pull request.",
    ex:"“/code-review ultra” before merging something that matters.",
    note:"<b>This is Claude Code's own, not Matt's.</b> Matt ships a skill with the identical name, and installing it silently shadowed this one, which would have cost you <b>/code-review ultra</b> (the cloud review that reproduces every bug before reporting it). His is kept updated in the database but deliberately switched off." },
  { id:"tdd", cat:"Building",
    what:"Writes the test first, watches it fail, then writes the code that makes it pass.",
    ex:"“Build the scoring logic test-first.”",
    note:"Worth it for anything where a silent wrong answer would be bad: scoring, progress, grading." },
  { id:"diagnosing-bugs", cat:"Building",
    what:"A proper hunt for a hard bug, instead of guessing and re-guessing.",
    ex:"“Students say the drill won't submit. Diagnose it.”",
    note:"For the bugs that survive the first three fixes, and for things that got mysteriously slow." },

  // ---- Writing ----
  { id:"writing-fragments", cat:"Writing",
    what:"Stage 1. Dumps out raw material and ideas with no structure yet.",
    ex:"“I want to write about why students misread conditionals. Get it all out.”",
    note:"Deliberately messy. Don't try to organise here." },
  { id:"writing-shape", cat:"Writing",
    what:"Stage 2. Shapes that raw material into an actual piece, paragraph by paragraph.",
    ex:"“Turn those fragments into a real explainer.”" },
  { id:"writing-beats", cat:"Writing",
    what:"Stage 3. Orders it into a journey, making sure nothing is used before it's explained.",
    ex:"“Check this teaches in the right order.”",
    note:"The one that catches you leaning on a term you haven't defined yet. The most common way teaching writing loses people." },
  { id:"writing-for-agents", cat:"Writing",
    what:"Writing instructions for Claude itself: skills, CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md.",
    ex:"“Rewrite this skill so it triggers properly.”",
    note:"<b>Directly useful to you.</b> This is the skill for everything we changed today." },

  // ---- Shape of the code ----
  { id:"domain-modeling", cat:"Code shape", type:"engine",
    what:"Pins down what your project's words actually mean, and records big decisions.",
    ex:"“Is a ‘drill’ the same thing as an ‘activity’? Decide and write it down.”",
    note:"Runs inside grill-with-docs. Its rule: the glossary is <b>only</b> a glossary, never a spec, never notes." },
  { id:"codebase-design", cat:"Code shape", type:"engine",
    what:"Shared vocabulary for designing a piece of code with a simple outside and a complex inside.",
    ex:"“Where should the line be between the drill engine and the UI?”" },
  { id:"improve-codebase-architecture", cat:"Code shape",
    what:"Scans the whole codebase for things worth improving and shows you a visual report.",
    ex:"“What's the messiest part of LSAT U right now?”",
    note:"You pick one from the report, then it grills you through fixing it." },
  { id:"research", cat:"Code shape",
    what:"Researches a question against real sources and saves the findings as a file.",
    ex:"“How does Supabase handle row-level security? Write it up.”",
    note:"Sticks to primary sources, so it's for facts you'll rely on, not a quick look." },
  { id:"triage", cat:"Code shape",
    what:"Takes a vague bug report or request and turns it into something workable.",
    ex:"“A student said ‘it's broken’, so work out what they mean.”" },

  // ---- Sessions ----
  { id:"handoff", cat:"Sessions", scope:"everywhere", type:"you type it",
    what:"Writes a file so a different session, or a different tool, can pick the work up.",
    ex:"Moving from Claude to Codex mid-project.",
    note:"Only for when work has to <b>travel</b>: another tool, another folder, another person. You have your own <b>session-handoff</b> that copies to the clipboard instead, which avoids this one's habit of hiding the file in a temp folder." },
  { id:"claude-handoff", cat:"Sessions",
    what:"Hands the work to a fresh background agent that starts immediately.",
    ex:"“Keep going on this while I do something else.”" },
  { id:"retro", cat:"Sessions",
    what:"Looks back at the session and works out what went wrong and what to change.",
    ex:"After a session that went badly, like the one that started all this." },
  { id:"wait-what", cat:"Sessions", scope:"everywhere",
    what:"Tells Claude that what it just said didn't land, and to explain it again differently.",
    ex:"“Wait, what?”",
    note:"<b>Use this one.</b> Faster than typing out that you're confused, it just re-pitches." },

  // ---- Setup & safety ----
  { id:"git-guardrails-claude-code", cat:"Setup",
    what:"Blocks dangerous git commands before they can run.",
    ex:"Stops a bad push or a hard reset wiping your work.",
    note:"<b>Worth running on lsatu.</b> It physically prevents the commands that lose code." },
  { id:"setup-matt-pocock-skills", cat:"Setup",
    what:"One-time setup so the other skills know where your tickets and docs live.",
    ex:"Run once in lsatu before wayfinder or triage.",
    note:"Wayfinder needs this. Without it, it falls back to plain markdown files." },
  { id:"setup-pre-commit", cat:"Setup",
    what:"Makes your project check itself automatically before every commit.",
    ex:"Catches formatting and type errors before they reach main." },
  { id:"resolving-merge-conflicts", cat:"Setup",
    what:"Walks you through a merge conflict that's already happening.",
    ex:"“I'm stuck in a rebase, get me out.”" },
  { id:"wizard", cat:"Setup",
    what:"Builds a step-by-step walkthrough for things only you can do by hand.",
    ex:"“Walk me through setting up the Stripe keys.”",
    note:"For clicking around someone else's dashboard, the stuff an agent can't do for you." }
];

const GROUPS = [
  { key:"Thinking",   title:"Thinking it through",  sub:"Before anything gets built. This is where your grill-me lives." },
  { key:"Building",   title:"Building it",          sub:"The chain from a settled conversation to reviewed code." },
  { key:"Writing",    title:"Writing",              sub:"Three stages, in order, plus one for writing instructions to Claude." },
  { key:"Code shape", title:"Shape of the code",    sub:"Understanding and improving what already exists." },
  { key:"Sessions",   title:"Surviving long sessions", sub:"For when the conversation gets too long, or has to move." },
  { key:"Setup",      title:"Setup and safety",     sub:"Run once, then forget. The guardrails are the valuable part." }
];

const CHAIN = [
  { id:"grill-with-docs", what:"decide what you're building" },
  { id:"to-spec",         what:"write it down" },
  { id:"to-tickets",      what:"break it into pieces" },
  { id:"implement",       what:"build each piece" },
  { id:"code-review",     what:"check it before merging (Claude Code\u2019s own)" }
];

const byId = Object.fromEntries(SKILLS.map(s => [s.id, s]));
let activeCat = "All";
let query = "";

/* ---- chips ---- */
const chipsEl = document.getElementById("chips");
["All", ...GROUPS.map(g => g.key)].forEach(k => {
  const b = document.createElement("button");
  b.className = "chip";
  b.type = "button";
  b.textContent = k === "All" ? "Everything" : (GROUPS.find(g => g.key === k)?.title ?? k);
  b.setAttribute("aria-pressed", k === "All" ? "true" : "false");
  b.addEventListener("click", () => {
    activeCat = k;
    [...chipsEl.children].forEach(c => c.setAttribute("aria-pressed", "false"));
    b.setAttribute("aria-pressed", "true");
    render();
  });
  chipsEl.appendChild(b);
});

/* ---- chain ---- */
const flow = document.getElementById("chainflow");
CHAIN.forEach((step, i) => {
  const b = document.createElement("button");
  b.className = "step";
  b.type = "button";
  b.innerHTML =
    '<span class="step-n">STEP ' + (i + 1) + '</span>' +
    '<span class="step-name">/' + step.id + '</span>' +
    '<span class="step-what">' + step.what + '</span>';
  b.addEventListener("click", () => openCard(step.id));
  flow.appendChild(b);
});

/* ---- cards ---- */
function cardHTML(s) {
  const tags =
    (s.yours ? '<span class="tag yours">yours</span>' : '') +
    (s.type ? '<span class="tag type">' + s.type + '</span>' : '') +
    (s.scope ? '<span class="tag scope">' + s.scope + '</span>' : '');
  return '' +
    '<article class="card" id="card-' + s.id + '">' +
      '<button class="cardbtn" type="button" aria-expanded="false">' +
        '<span class="cardtop"><span class="cmd">/' + s.id + '</span>' + tags +
          '<span class="caret" aria-hidden="true">+</span></span>' +
        '<p class="what">' + s.what + '</p>' +
      '</button>' +
      '<div class="detail">' +
        '<div class="example"><span class="lbl">You might say</span><p>' + s.ex + '</p></div>' +
        (s.note ? '<p class="note">' + s.note + '</p>' : '') +
      '</div>' +
    '</article>';
}

function render() {
  const q = query.trim().toLowerCase();
  const main = document.getElementById("groups");
  let shown = 0;
  let html = "";

  GROUPS.forEach(g => {
    if (activeCat !== "All" && activeCat !== g.key) return;
    const items = SKILLS.filter(s =>
      s.cat === g.key &&
      (!q || (s.id + " " + s.what + " " + s.ex + " " + (s.note || "")).toLowerCase().includes(q))
    );
    if (!items.length) return;
    shown += items.length;
    html +=
      '<section class="group">' +
        '<div class="grouphead"><h2>' + g.title + '</h2><span class="n">' + items.length + '</span></div>' +
        '<p class="groupsub">' + g.sub + '</p>' +
        '<div class="cards">' + items.map(cardHTML).join("") + '</div>' +
      '</section>';
  });

  main.innerHTML = html || '<p class="empty">Nothing matches &ldquo;' + query + '&rdquo;.</p>';
  document.getElementById("count").textContent = shown + " of " + SKILLS.length;

  main.querySelectorAll(".cardbtn").forEach(btn => {
    btn.addEventListener("click", () => {
      const card = btn.closest(".card");
      const open = card.classList.toggle("is-open");
      btn.setAttribute("aria-expanded", open ? "true" : "false");
    });
  });
}

function openCard(id) {
  query = ""; activeCat = "All";
  document.getElementById("q").value = "";
  [...chipsEl.children].forEach((c, i) => c.setAttribute("aria-pressed", i === 0 ? "true" : "false"));
  render();
  const card = document.getElementById("card-" + id);
  if (!card) return;
  card.classList.add("is-open");
  card.querySelector(".cardbtn").setAttribute("aria-expanded", "true");
  card.scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth", block: "center" });
}

document.getElementById("q").addEventListener("input", e => { query = e.target.value; render(); });
render();
</script>

</body></html>
</cowritten-artifact-html>

grill-me is the user-facing command that activates it. <a class="related" data-skill="grill-me" href="#skill-grill-me">See /grill-me →</a></p></div><div class="detail-part companion"><h4>Related skills</h4><div class="companion-links"><a class="related" data-skill="grill-me" href="#skill-grill-me">/grill-me ↗</a><a class="related" data-skill="grill-with-docs" href="#skill-grill-with-docs">/grill-with-docs ↗</a><a class="related" data-skill="wayfinder" href="#skill-wayfinder">/wayfinder ↗</a></div></div><div class="detail-part"><h4>Important distinction</h4><p>This is the reusable interview discipline. /grill-me and /grill-with-docs are the usual user-facing ways to enter it.</p></div><div class="workflow-links"><h4>See it in a workflow</h4><div class="support-links"><button class="support-chip" data-flow="feature" type="button">Build a larger feature ↗</button><button class="support-chip" data-flow="architecture" type="button">Clean up a painful area ↗</button></div></div><div class="detail-bottom"><a href="https://github.com/mattpocock/skills/blob/b0618bc436ad893b3c5e84e55fba86586d34a404/skills/productivity/grilling/SKILL.md" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read original SKILL.md <span aria-hidden="true">↗</span></a><a href="https://www.aihero.dev/skills-grilling" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Matt’s guide ↗</a><span>Productivity collection</span></div></div></details><div class="quick-example"><div class="quick-head"><span>Sample trigger</span><button aria-label="Copy full grilling example" class="copy-btn mini-command" data-copy="/grilling

href="https://www.aihero.dev/5-agent-skills-i-use-every-day" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank"><span class="source-type">Video &amp; written companion · AI Hero</span><h4>5 Agent Skills I Use Older skill names may differ.</p></a><a class="source-card" href="https://www.aihero.dev/my-grill-me-skill-has-gone-viral" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank"><span class="source-type">Video &amp; written companion · AI Hero</span><h4>My “Grill Me” Skill Went Viral <span class="arrow">↗</span></h4><p>Background on using the interview pattern before committing to a plan.</p></a></section></div><aside aria-label="Practical guidance" class="source-tips"><article class="reading-tip"><h4>Do not restart completed work</h4><p>A ready ticket can enter at /implement. An already-built change can enter at /code-review. A small agreed change need not become a multi-session planning project.</p><a href="https://www.aihero.dev/skills-implement" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read the source ↗</a></article><article class="reading-tip"><h4>Do not run every box manually</h4><p>The user-invoked steps need your command. Helper skills, such as TDD and review inside implementation, are not extra compulsory stages.</p><a href="https://github.com/mattpocock/skills/blob/b0618bc436ad893b3c5e84e55fba86586d34a404/.agents/invocation.md" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read the source ↗</a></article><article class="reading-tip"><h4>Prototype questions you need to see</h4><p>Use a runnable experiment for a layout or state behavior you cannot judge through conversation. Bring the result back into the design discussion.</p><a href="https://www.aihero.dev/things-people-get-wrong-with-grill-me-and-grill-with-docs" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read the source ↗</a></article><article class="reading-tip"><h4>Keep the decisions before clearing</h4><p>Capture settled decisions in the spec and tickets before throwing away their context. A handoff is especially useful when context must travel to another tool or workspace.</p><a href="https://github.com/mattpocock/skills/blob/b0618bc436ad893b3c5e84e55fba86586d34a404/skills/engineering/ask-matt/SKILL.md" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read the source ↗</a></article><article class="reading-tip"><h4>Check which diff was reviewed</h4><p>The current code-review command reads &lt;base&gt;...HEAD. Uncommitted work is outside that comparison. Confirm that the latest intended changes are actually in the reviewed diff.</p><a href="https://www.aihero.dev/skills-code-review" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read the source ↗</a></article><article class="reading-tip"><h4>Keep custom rules outside managed copies</h4><p>Matt’s guide recommends standing project behavior in your own AGENTS.md or CLAUDE.md, or in the invocation. Editing a managed skill copy can be lost on update.</p><a href="https://www.aihero.dev/skills-ask-matt" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">Read the source ↗</a></article></aside></div><section class="provenance"><h3>What is verified, and what is adapted?</h3><p><strong>Repository snapshot:</strong> <code>b0618bc436ad893b3c5e84e55fba86586d34a404</code>, checked October 8, 2026. The repository contains 38 SKILL.md files; the <a href="https://github.com/mattpocock/skills/blob/b0618bc436ad893b3c5e84e55fba86586d34a404/.claude-plugin/plugin.json" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank">core plugin manifest</a> lists 27. The other 11 are seven in-progress and four misc skills. This is a snapshot, not a live updater or installer.</p><p><strong>Source behavior:</strong> Every card links to its exact pinned SKILL.md. First-party guides add practical context. Where older material differs, use the current skill instructions. For example, the earlier catalog said to discard a prototype; the current source instead captures it on a separate branch as reference evidence. The description has been corrected and labelled accordingly.</p><p><strong>Adapted examples:</strong> The LSAT U situations, prompts, 13 workflow layouts, and 28 starting-point choices are authored examples. They do not claim to be Matt’s own projects or verbatim recommendations. Labelled “documented routes” follow his routing guidance; labelled “adapted recipes” combine skills for these example tasks.</p><p><strong>Video coverage:</strong> The YouTube links were located and the available titles and descriptions checked. Full YouTube transcripts were not available here. Explanations are grounded in the readable skill files and first-party guides, not claims about unseen video passages. No paywalled course or private account material was accessed.</p><p><strong>Your files:</strong> This replaces neither your installed skills nor your customized copies. It only updates the downloadable catalog. Browsing, diagrams, search, and examples work offline; external sources and videos need an internet connection. The underlying repository is MIT licensed.</p></section></section><footer class="footer">Matt Pocock skills · Independent interactive field guide · Snapshot checked October 8, 2026 at <code>b0618bc</code>. NEW 215 function promptHTML(skill,index,prefix){const ex=examples[skill][index];return `<div class="prompt-wrap"><div class="prompt-top"><span>Example prompt � edit the details</span><div class="prompt-actions"><button class="copy-btn copy-skill-btn" type="button" data-copy-skill="${esc(skill)}" aria-label="Copy /${esc(skill)} skill command" title="Copies the skill command only">Copy skill</button><button class="copy-btn" type="button" data-copy="${esc(ex.prompt)}" aria-label="Copy ${esc(skill)} example">Copy example</button></div></div><pre class="prompt-text" id="${esc(prefix)}">${esc(ex.prompt)}</pre></div><p class="result-line"><strong>You get</strong>${esc(ex.result)}</p>`}

NEW 286 async function copy(text,button,kind='example'){let ok=false;try{if(navigator.clipboard&&window.isSecureContext){await navigator.clipboard.writeText(text);ok=true}}catch(e){}
